<!-- page 1 -->
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, March 13, 2018
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, March 13, 2018, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive
session to discuss certain matters as indicated on the following agenda. The Board may meet in executive session pursuant to
A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for legal advice on any item.
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s office
at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting so an accommodation can be arranged.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, with the exception of items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo
County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up to 5
minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.
 
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature
and will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be
removed from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: February 27, 2018  
 
b. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 February 2018  
 
c. Clerk of Superior Court Report February 2018  
 
d. Juvenile Probation Report February 2018  
 
e. Constable Reports: Winslow Precinct #2, January; Kayenta Precinct #4, February; Show Low
Precinct #5, February; and Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6, February 2018
 
 
f. Justice Court Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1,  Winslow Precinct #2, Snowflake Precinct #3,
Kayenta Precinct #4, Show Low Precinct #5, Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6, February 2018
 
 
g. Personnel Actions  
 
h. Adoption of Resolution 04-18 naming Catrina Jenkins as the Navajo County Applicant Agent
for all disasters 
 
 
i. Update to the Arizona Mutual Aid Compact Appendix B - Points of Contact

<!-- page 2 -->
j. Adopt Resolution Number 05-18, canceling the elections for White Mountain Summer Home
Domestic Water Improvement District and appointing the candidate(s) or deeming the position
vacant
 
 
k. Request use of RICO Funds for Winslow Little League  
 
l. Grant Award Contract for Grant Number CNA18-502 in the amount of $4,172.42 for
attendance to National Conference Training from the State of Arizona Constable Ethics,
Standards & Training Board
 
 
m. Grant Award Contract for Grant Number CNA18-402 in the amount of $26,662.52 for Vehicles
from the State of Arizona Constable Ethics, Standards & Training Board
 
 
3. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
 
a. Recognition of the Winslow Boys' Basketball Team for winning the 2018 3A State Basketball
Championship.
 
Presented By: Supervisor Jesse Thompson
 
b. Consideration of and approval to appoint John Flynn, Policy Logic, LLC, as District
Administrator for the White Mountain Lakes Fire District and approve contract for Mr. Flynn's
services. 
 
Presented By: Jason Moore/John Flynn
 
4. COUNTY ATTORNEY:  
 
a. Consideration and acceptance of FY19 ACJC National Criminal History Improvement Grant
Agreement #NCP-17-18-007.
 
Presented By: Lynda Young
 
5. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board wishes
to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
1. Professional services agreement with Connie Baine, RDH for Oral Health Coalition
Development services covering Navajo, Apache, Gila Counties effective March 1, 2018 through
February 29, 2019 in the amount of $45.00/hr for 216 hours
 
 
2. Professional services agreement with Regina Rich, RDH for Oral Health Coalition
Development services covering Navajo, Apache & Gila Counties effective March 1, 2018
through February 29, 2019 in the amount of $45.00/hr for 246 hours

<!-- page 3 -->
6. INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY:  
 
a. Presentation on projects that the Information Technology Department is working on for the
2017-2018 budget year.
 
Presented By: Ken Dewitt
 
7. PUBLIC WORKS:  
 
a. Consideration of award of contract #B18-01-019 Supply of Various Roadway Materials to
Perkins Cinders, Inc., Hatch Construction and Paving, Inc., Brimhall Sand and Rock and
Building Materials, Inc., Cholla Ready Mix, Inc., and Dyna Rock and Sand, Inc. 
 
Presented By: John Osgood
 
8. WHITE MOUNTAIN LAKES COUNTY RECREATION IMPROVEMENT DISTRICT:
Board of Directors Session:
 
 
a. REGULAR AGENDA  
 
1. Consideration and approval of Updated Rules and Regulations Manual.  
Presented By: Pat Stevens/Melissa Buckley
 
9. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and Board
members.
 
 
 
10. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: March 8, 2018 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 11:00 p.m. By: MB                         
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD 
OF SUPERVISORS, DESIGNATING THE EMERGENCY MANAGER AS 
APPLICANT’S AGENT FOR ARIZONA DIVISION OF EMERGENCY 
MANAGEMENT 
 
WHEREAS, the State of Arizona’s Administrative Plan for Public Assistance provides in 
paragraph VII.B.2.b that in emergencies for which financial assistance may be available, the 
applicant’s Chief Elected Official shall designate in writing an agent who will be the point of 
contact in all matters pertaining to applications for assistance and that if no agent is designated, 
the Chief Elected Official shall automatically be designated as the applicant’s agent; and 
 
WHEREAS, the designation of the applicant’s agent must be made on the Arizona 
Division of Emergency Managements Form #AZ PA 204-04; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County finds that it would be 
appropriate to designate Catrina Jenkins as applicant’s agent for Navajo County in its dealings 
with the Arizona Division of Emergency Management; 
 
 NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED by the Board of Supervisors as follows: 
 
1. Catrina Jenkins is hereby designated as applicant’s agent for Navajo County in its 
dealings with Arizona Division of Emergency Management, such designation to continue 
until further notice to ADEM. 
 
2. The Chairman is hereby authorized to execute and deliver to ADEM a completed Form 
#AZ PA 204-04 designating Mary Springer as applicant’s agent. 
  
PASSED AND ADOPTED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors on March 13, 
2018.

Submitted By: Rayleen Richards, Elections Director

Motion before the Board:
Adopt Resolution Number 05-18, canceling the elections for White Mountain Summer Home
Domestic Water Improvement District and appointing the candidate(s) or deeming the position
vacant
Background:
48-1012. Election of trustees; vacancies
 
E. If only one person files or no person files a nominating petition for election to fill a position on
the board of directors of the district, the county board of supervisors, by resolution, may cancel
the election for that office and appoint the person who filed the nominating petition to fill that
position. If no person files a nominating petition for an election to fill a district board office, the
county board of supervisors, by resolution, may cancel the election for those offices and those
offices are deemed vacant and shall be filled as otherwise provided by law. A person who is
appointed pursuant to this section is fully vested with the powers and duties of the office as if
elected to that office.

RESOLUTION NO ______ - 18 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS, CANCELLING THE ELECTIONS FOR 
WHITE MOUNTIAN SUMMER HOME DWID; AND APPOINTING THE CANDIDATE(S) OR DEEMING THE 
POSITION VACANT, PURSUANT TO ARS § 48-1012 (E) 
 
 
 
WHEREAS, ARS § 48-1012 (E) provides as follows with respect to Domestic Water Improvement District 
Board of Directors positions: 
 
“If only one person files or no person files a nominating petition for election to fill a position on the board of 
directors of the district, the county board of supervisors, by resolution, may cancel the election for that office and 
appoint the person who filed the nominating petition to fill that position. If no person files a nominating petition for an 
election to fill a district board office, the county board of supervisors, by resolution, may cancel the election for those 
offices and those offices are deemed vacant and shall be filled as otherwise provided by law. A person who is 
appointed pursuant to this section is fully vested with the powers and duties of the office as if elected to that office.” 
 
         and 
 
WHEREAS, A.R.S. § 16-410 (A) authorizes the Board to take such action no earlier than seventy-five (75) 
days before the election, and it is now less than seventy-five (75) days before May 15, 2018 Special Election at which 
the Domestic Water Improvement Districts Board of Directors will be elected; 
 
         and 
 
WHEREAS, the officer in charge of elections, has certified that the number of persons who filed nominating 
petitions to fill the Domestic Water Improvement Districts Board of Directors positions is less than or equal to the 
number of positions available, attached hereto and incorporated herein as “Exhibit A”; 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED by the Board of Supervisors as follows:  
 
That the elections for the White Mountain Summer Home DWID Board of Directors positions, attached hereto 
and incorporated herein as “Exhibit A”, are hereby cancelled by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors.

Board of Supervisors Regular 3.b.        
Meeting Date: 03/13/2018  
Title: Appointment of District Administrator for White Mountain Lakes Fire District
Submitted For: Jason Moore, Deputy County Attorney 
Submitted By: Jason Moore, Deputy County Attorney
Department: County Attorney
Presented By: Jason Moore/John Flynn
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of and approval to appoint John Flynn, Policy Logic, LLC, as District
Administrator for the White Mountain Lakes Fire District and approve contract for Mr. Flynn's
services. 
Background:
The White Mountain Lakes Fire District is governed by a five-member Board.  Due to previous
unfilled resignation on the Board, as well as two new resignations that recently occurred, the
Board is now unable to form a quorum to conduct District business.
A.R.S. Section 48-803 addresses the responsibilities of the Board of Supervisors when a fire
district governing board falls below the number of members required for a quorum.
B. If a vacancy occurs on the district board other than from expiration of a term, a quorum of the
board members shall fill the vacancy by appointment of an interim member and except for a
district formed pursuant to article 3 of this chapter, a quorum of the board members shall fill the
vacancy within ninety days after the date the vacancy occurs. Except for a district formed pursuant
to article 3 of this chapter, if the quorum of district board members do not appoint an interim
member within that ninety-day period, the board of supervisors shall appoint an interim member
to the district board within sixty days after expiration of the ninety-day period, and if the district is
located in more than one county, the board of supervisors of the county in which the majority of
the assessed valuation of the district is located shall make the appointment after the expiration of
the ninety-day period.   If the entire board resigns or for any reason cannot fulfill its duties,
or does not have remaining board members sufficient to constitute a quorum of the board,
the board of supervisors shall appoint interim district board members to fill the vacancies,
or an administrator to administer the district with the same duties and obligations of the
elected board to serve until the next election. A majority of the board constitutes a quorum for
the transaction of business.
In addition to lacking a quorum on the District governing Board, the District's Chief is presently
on paid administrative leave, and the Assistant and Interim Chief has resigned his position.  The
appointment of Mr. Flynn as District Administrator is recommended to the Board of Supervisors
so that necessary District business can be conducted, necessary personnel decisions can be made,

<!-- page 414 -->
and to stabilize the District.
Mr. Flynn was previously appointed District Administrator for this fire district in 2009 when the
District previously encountered difficulties.  During that appointment, Mr. Flynn successfully led
the District out of serious financial difficulties and brought stability to the District until it was
ready for self-governance.  He is familiar with the District and the area, and it is believed that the
appointment of Mr. Flynn is the best option available under the circumstances.  Mr. Flynn is
closely associated with the Arizona Fire Districts Association, and has done similar work for other
fire districts around the State of Arizona.
Recommendation: 
Recommend appointing Mr. John Flynn, Policy Logic, LLC, as the District Administrator for the
White Mountain Lakes Fire District.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year:
Budgeted Y/N:
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
The fee is $3,500.00 monthly to be paid for from the District's general fund. Necessary travel
expenses incurred by Consultant in connection with this agreement will be billed at the Internal
Revenue Service standard mileage rate and accounted for monthly. Lodging expenses if necessary
will be billed at the state employee’s contract government rate.

PolicyLogic – Fire & EMS Consulting Services 
 
Navajo Co – WMLFD 2018  Page 1 of 4 
 
PROPOSAL:   Navajo County  
100 East Code Talkers Drive 
South Highway 77  
P.O. Box 668  
Holbrook, AZ 86025 
PROJECT APPRAISAL 
The White Mountain Lake Fire District (the “District”) is currently without elected official governance 
due to the resignation of board members. The Navajo County Board of Supervisors (the “County”) seeks 
to appoint a District Administrator with the same duties and obligations of an elected fire district board 
pursuant to Arizona Revised Statute (ARS) §48-803(B). The County seeks to appoint an experienced 
administrator to facilitate the effective and efficient operation of the District in accordance with Arizona 
law. The County therefore seeks to appoint John Flynn, as principal member of the firm of PolicyLogic 
(the “Consultant”) to serve as Administrator of the District pursuant to ARS §48-803(B).   
PROJECT OBJECTIVES 
Throughout the duration of this Agreement the Consultant, serving as District Administrator, shall 
assume, “the same duties and obligations of the elected board,” pursuant to A.R.S. § 48-803(B). Those 
obligations are specifically set forth in ARS §48-805 et.al. and shall include:   
• Holding at a minimum one public meeting every month to discuss / consider fire district business 
and issues as required by law. 
• Governance and financial oversight / management of the District to include: 
o Ensuring short term and long-term financial plans are provided pursuant to statutory 
requirements. 
o Approval of the annual budget including conducting public hearings and ensuring 
compliance with the provisions of A.R.S. § 48-805(A)(2). 
o Ongoing oversight of the District’s administrative, governance and financial responsibilities 
as required by law. 
o Establish public policy specific to the delivery of fire, emergency medical and associated 
emergency services for the District and facilitate the effective and efficient delivery of said 
services within the limits of resources annually allocated to the District. 
PROJECT SCOPE / DELIVERABLES   
The work product to be delivered for this project includes:   
• Ongoing oversight and compliance of the affairs of the District during the term of the appointment 
in adherence to applicable law. 
• Provide a complete financial accounting of the District and its’ affairs to the County and the 
taxpayers of the District on a monthly basis. 
• Attend meetings, present and submit reports to the County and or the District’s Citizens as 
necessary. 
• In conjunction with the County, establish the property tax liability and property tax levy for the 
District and its’ taxpayers for the fiscal year beginning July 1, 2018 and subsequent fiscal years as 
required by law.

JOINT ACCOUNTABILITIES   
The County will be responsible to provide reasonable access to past and current District records, 
documentation, information and data that would aid in achieving the project objectives, along with 
reasonable access to key personnel.    
The Consultant will provide governance, financial and administrative oversight of the District’s affairs 
pursuant to statutory requirements, which would primarily occur off-site. The Consultant will conduct a 
majority of responsibilities from the Phoenix metropolitan area. The Consultant will attend meetings 
and be on-site at the County and District facilities as necessary to ensure the affairs of the District are 
conducted appropriately.     
The Consultant and the County agree to immediately appraise each other of any information or findings 
that would impact the success of the project so that rapid action could be considered.        
FEE FOR SERVICE   
The fee for this project is $3,500.00 monthly. The monthly fee will be paid from the District’s general 
fund with invoices presented monthly to the Navajo County Attorney’s Office for review prior to 
payment.   
Necessary travel expenses incurred by Consultant in connection with this agreement will be billed at the 
Internal Revenue Service standard mileage rate and accounted for monthly. Lodging expenses if 
necessary will be billed at the state employee’s contract government rate.    
TERM OF AGREEMENT   
The Administrator appointment by the County is anticipated to occur March 13, 2017. The Administrator 
shall serve from the date of appointment until a governing board is seated by either special election or 
general election as determined by the County. 
Either party has the right to terminate this Agreement at will after giving a 60 day advance written 
notice to the non-terminating party. Payment for services rendered by the Consultant prior to 
termination of the Agreement will be prorated on a daily basis. The County may terminate this 
Agreement pursuant to ARS §38-511. 
The Consultant, upon termination of this agreement, shall turn over all financial records of the District, 
as well as any District property in the Consultant’s possession, to the District. All work product of the 
Administrator shall become the property of the District, and shall be turned over to the District at that 
time. The Consultant may retain a copy of district records and work product for his own records as 
allowed by law.   
TERMS AND CONDITIONS   
The Consultant shall be covered by the District’s Public Officials Professional Liability insurance policy 
throughout the term of the appointment with an aggregate limit of not less than $1,000,000.    
To the fullest extent permitted by law, the County will defend, indemnify and hold harmless the 
Consultant , its authorized agents, authorized representatives and employees from and against injury or 
death of any person, loss or damage to any property or any allegations, demands, proceedings, suits,

shall be reported to the government at the end of each calendar year by County. It is understood that

ASSIGNMENT   
This Agreement is entered into in reliance upon and in consideration of the singular skill and

obligation without such consent shall be void.

Board of Supervisors Regular 4.a.        
Meeting Date: 03/13/2018  
Title: ACJC National Criminal History Improvement Grant
Submitted For: Brad Carlyon, County Attorney  Submitted By: Lynda Young, Office
Manager
Department: County Attorney
Presented By: Lynda Young
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and acceptance of FY19 ACJC National Criminal History Improvement Grant
Agreement #NCP-17-18-007.
Background:
Perhaps the biggest miscarriage of justice is for someone who has been arrested for a crime,
proved to have committed the crime, but because there isn’t a good set of fingerprints, may be
exonerated for the crime.  Or, the criminal actually served time, but because the fingerprints are
not recorded properly, the crime would be missing from the criminal’s history.  That means that
criminals are walking around with incomplete criminal histories.  They could re-offend, and
because of the gap, there are crimes they have committed that are not recorded.  Histories that are
critical to know when the offender is arrested. 
 
How does this happen?  During normal case flow process, an offender is ordered to get
fingerprinted prior to coming to Court.  If the defendant fails to get fingerprinted, the Court does
not always check to see if fingerprints for this crime are on file.  The defendant is sentenced,
serves a sentence, but the criminal history is never complete because of missing fingerprints.
 
This project addresses a gap in the fingerprint process.  Navajo County is proposing to work with
the existing ACJC Criminal Justice Records Improvement Program grant in the Superior Courts. 
In the CJRIP, when a defendant is in Court during Sentencing, the defendant will undergo a
two-digit identifier (2FID) to confirm a 10-print record is on file for the crime in Court. 
 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval of grant agreement.

systems to improve the functioning of the cr iminal justice system, with an emphasis 
on integration of all criminal justice components, law enforcement, courts,  
prosecution, corrections, and probation and parole. Further, the strategy must be 
developed in consultation with State and local officials w ith emphasis on the 
recommendation of officials whose duty it is to oversee, plan, and implement 
integrated information technology systems, and contain -- (a) a definition and 
analysis of integration in the State and localities developing integrated informa tion 
sharing systems; (b) an assessment of the criminal justice resources being devoted 
to information technology; (c) State and local resource needs; (d) Federal, State, 
regional, and local information technology coordination requirements; and (e) 
statewide priorities for planning and implementation of  information technology 
systems.

covenants contained in the Intergovernmental Agreement.  The State of Arizona  in no way warrants that 
the minimum limits contained herein are sufficient to protect the governmental entity or Contractor from 
liabilities that might arise out of the performance of the work under this Contract by the Contractor, his 
agents, representatives, employees or subcontractors, and Contractor and the governmental entity are 
free to purchase additional insurance.   
 
A. MINIMUM SCOPE AND LIMITS OF INSURANCE:   Contractor shall provide coverage with  limits 
of liability not less than those stated below.

Additional Insurance Requirements: 
 
The policies shall include, or be endorsed to include, as required by this written agreement, the following 
provisions: 
 
The Contractor's policies shall stipulate that the insurance afforded the Contractor shall be primary and 
that any insurance carried by the Department, its agents, officials, employees or the State of Arizona 
shall be excess and not contributory insurance, as provided by A.R.S. § 41-621 (E). 
 
Insurance provided by the Contractor shall not limit the Contractor’s liability assumed under the 
indemnification provisions of this Contract. 
 
Notice of Cancellation: 
For each insurance policy required by the insurance provisions of this Contract, the Contractor must 
provide to the State of Arizona, within two (2) business days of receipt, a notice if a policy is suspended,

<!-- page 440 -->
National Criminal History Improvement Program ACJC Grant Number NCP 17-18-007 Page 20 
 
voided, or cancelled for any reason. Such notice shall be mailed, emailed, hand delivered or sent by 
facsimile transmission to (Enter Contracting Agency Representative’s Name, Address, and Fax Number 
Here). 
 
Acceptability of Insurers: 
Contractor’s insurance shall be placed with companies licensed in the State of Arizona or hold approved 
non-admitted status on the Arizona Department of Insurance List of Qualified Unauthorized Insurers. 
Insurers shall have an “A.M. Best” rating of not less than A- VII. The State of Arizona in no way warrants 
that the above-required minimum insurer rating is sufficient to protect the Contractor from potential 
insurer insolvency. 
 
Verification of Coverage: 
Contractor shall furnish the State of Arizona with certificates of insurance (valid ACORD form or 
equivalent approved by the State of Arizona) as required by this Contract. An authorized representative 
of the insurer shall sign the certificates. 
 
All certificates and endorsements, as required by this written agreement, are to be received and 
approved by the State of Arizona before work commences. Each insurance policy required by this 
Contract must be in effect at, or prior to, commencement of work under this Contract. Failure to maintain 
the insurance policies as required by this Contract, or to provide evidence of renewal, is a material breach 
of contract. 
 
All certificates required by this Contract shall be sent directly to the Department. The State of Arizona 
project/contract number and project description shall be noted on the certificate of insurance. The State 
of Arizona reserves the right to require complete copies of all insurance policies required by this Contract 
at any time. 
 
Subcontractors: 
Contractor’s certificate(s) shall include all subcontractors as insureds under its policies or Contractor shall 
be responsible for ensuring and/or verifying that all subcontractors have valid and collectable insurance 
as evidenced by the certificates of insurance and endorsements for each subcontractor. All coverages for 
subcontractors shall be subject to the minimum Insurance Requirements identified above. The 
Department reserves the right to require, at any time throughout the life of the Contract, proof from the 
Contractor that its subcontractors have the required coverage. 
 
Approval and Modifications: 
The Contracting Agency, in consultation with State Risk, reserves the right to review or make 
modifications to the insurance limits, required coverages, or endorsements throughout the life of this 
contract, as deemed necessary. Such action will not require a formal Contract amendment but may be 
made by administrative action. 
 
Exceptions: 
In the event the Contractor or subcontractor(s) is/are a public entity, then the Insurance Requirements 
shall not apply. Such public entity shall provide a certificate of self-insurance. If the Contractor or 
subcontractor(s) is/are a State of Arizona agency, board, commission, or university, none of the above 
shall apply.

Board of Supervisors Regular 7.a.        
Meeting Date: 03/13/2018  
Title: Award of Contract B18-01-019 Supply of Various Roadway Materials
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Cheryl Hollins, Account Specialist
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of contract #B18-01-019 Supply of Various Roadway Materials to Perkins
Cinders, Inc., Hatch Construction and Paving, Inc., Brimhall Sand and Rock and Building
Materials, Inc., Cholla Ready Mix, Inc., and Dyna Rock and Sand, Inc. 
Background:
Sealed proposals were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Governmental
Center, Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 p.m. M.S.T, February 28, 2018, in strict accordance with the
specifications on file in the office of the Navajo County Public Works.  No bids were accepted
after 3:00 p.m. M.S.T.  The bids were publicly opened and read aloud at 3:15 p.m.  This is a
annual "multi-award" contract for roadway materials which are purchased based on price,
convenience, availability and/or quality.  The award of contracts to all bidders allows adequate
coverage for road materials by location.
Recommendation: 
The unit prices have decreased approximately .04 cents per ton for all materials with the
exception of 2 sack slurry and concrete which has an average increase of $5.31 per cubic yard. 
The 2 sack slurry and concrete materials are not utilized as often as the other materials. With
these being our local vendors, staff is recommending award of contracts to all bidders.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY 2018
Budgeted Y/N: Yes
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
The Public Works budget includes funding for road maintenance materials.

CONTRACT 
 
THIS CONTRACT is made and entered into as of March 28, 2018 (the 
“Effective Date”) by and between Navajo County (“Coun ty”) and Perkins Cinders, Inc.  
(“Contractor”). 
 
1. SCOPE OF WORK : Contractor shall furnish any and all  materials, equipment and 
labor required for performing all work for Supplying Various Roadway Materials, 
Contract # B18-01-019, in accordance with the Contract Documents, in a workmanlike 
manner to the satisfaction of County through its Engineers. 
 
2. CONTRACT DOCUMENTS: The Contract Documents comprise this Contract and the 
attached ☒ Call for Bids, ☐ Specifications, ☐ General Provisions, ☒ Special 
Provisions, ☒ Proposal dated February 26, 2018, ☒ Bidding Schedule, ☒ Bid Bond, 
☐ Performance Bond, ☒ No Collusion Affidavit, ☒ Subcontracting Certification, ☐ 
Plans, and ☐ addenda thereto, if any.  If there is any conflict in the documents, this 
Contract shall control over all others; the Specifications shall control over the General 
Provisions and Special Provisions; and the Special Provisions shall control over the 
General Provisions. 
 
3. NO LIENS / TIME FOR COMPLETION : Contractor shall furnish all materials, 
equipment and labor in accordance with the Contract Documents free and clear of all 
claims, liens and charges whatsoever, within the time, or times, stated in the Proposal. 
 
4. CANCELLATION: This Contract is subject to cancellation pursuant to A.R.S. § 38 -
511, (concerning conflicts of interest). 
 
5. PAYMENTS:  Payment shall be mad e on the basis of the unit price(s) bid for the 
awarded items in the Bidding Schedule for Contract #  B18-01-019.  Contractor shall 
submit invoices to County for all work performed hereunder.  No later than 30 days 
after each invoice has been verified and approved by the Engineer, County shall pay 
to Contractor 100% of the approved amount. 
 
6. CERTIFICATIONS: 
 
A. Pursuant to A.R.S. § 41 -4401, Contractor and its subcontractors warrant 
compliance with all federal immigration laws and regulations relating to their 
employees and compliance with the E -verify requirements under A.R.S. § 23 -
214(A).  Contractor's or any subcontractor's breach of this warranty shall be 
deemed a material breach of the Contract and may result in termination of the 
Contract by County.  County may randomly inspect the books and records of 
Contractor and its subcontractors to verify compliance with this warranty.  
Contractor and its subcontractors warrant to keep their books and records open 
for random inspection by County during normal business hours.

deemed a material breach of the Contract and may result in termination of the 
Contract by County.  County may randomly inspect the books and records of 
Contractor and its subcontractors to verify compliance with this warranty.  
Contractor and its subcontractors warrant to keep their books and records open 
for random inspection by County during normal business hours.

Board of Supervisors Regular 8.a.1.        
Meeting Date: 03/13/2018  
Title: Rules & Regulations 2018
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Wht Mtn Lakes County Recreation Impr
Presented By: Pat Stevens/Melissa Buckley
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and approval of Updated Rules and Regulations Manual. 
Background:
November 2017, the District Administrator put out a notice to the community requesting feedback
on the current Rules and Regulations Manual. Upon receiving several comments, suggestions and
requests to be a part of a group to review this document; the Administrator, Thomas Aisenbrey,
Jessica DeLaney, Charles Fickel, Bob Riester, John Tobin, Mark Knoell and Melissa Buckley
reviewed the document in January 2018 and brought forth a draft for the community to review.
The draft was placed on the website in February and a town hall was held on March 1st with
community members, whom discussed the draft manual. Suggestion were made and incorporated
into the FINAL DRAFT that was placed on the website for further discussion. 
 
Recommendation: 
District Personnel, the community and County staff request Board approval.

WHITE MOUNTAIN LAKES 
COUNTY RECREATION IMPROVEMENT DISTRICT 
“White Mountain Lakes County Recreation Improvement District” is a Navajo County 
Improvement District established by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors in February 
1993 to manage and improve the recreational use of White Mountain Lake and Mexican 
Lake through a current agreement with the Silver Creek Irrigation District. 
 
A private, members only, recreation area 
 
2015 Silver Creek Drive 
Phone: (928) 532 -8099 
Website: WML-RecreationDistrict.org 
Email: whitemtnlake@frontier.com 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
RULES AND REGULATIONS 
 
Approved & Adopted by 
WMLCRID Board of Directors 
 
Revised 
March  2018

<!-- page 468 -->
2 
 
 
Emergencies….call 9-1-1 
The District takes or implies NO responsibility for personal injury of any kind, damage to or 
theft of personal property. The lake and facilities are used as is and at your own risk. 
 
1.0 Access and Operating Hours 
1.1 Access to the Lakes and areas owned, leased or controlled by the White Mountain Lakes County Recreation 
Improvement District is granted under the condition that the laws of the State of Arizona and these Rules 
and Regulations are  observed. Access is granted only to District Members and Associate Members. Any 
violation of either State Law or District Rules and Regulations can result in permanent or temporary 
revocation of access of the individual member. 
1.2 The Lakes will be open to Authorized Users at all times with certain restrictions to prevent conflict between 
fishing and water skiing. Restrictions: Excessive noise and excessive wake are not allowed on any day 
before 10:00 AM and after sunset. 
1.3 Use of District Facilities is at the users’ own risk. 
1.4 The District is not responsible for damage, loss, or theft of property. 
1.5 All persons using District facilities must display proof of access. Proof of access can be obtained at the 
lake office during business hours. 
A.  District Member: To receive your placard, you must go to the Lake Office and fill out a form with 
the following information: 
Member’s Name 
Member’s Phone Number(s) 
Email address 
Parcel # or address 
Current Mailing address 
Once your information is verified via the Navajo County Assessor’s website you will be issued your 
placard. Your placard may be shared with your family, guests, and renters. You take responsibility 
for those using your placard. Guests: If you have other gu ests joining you at the lake, you need to 
notify the District Personnel; identify yourself as a District Member (provide placard number, Email 
address, or phone number) and provide them with your guest’s information. Your guest(s) will need 
to stop at the office with identification and they will be given a day pass to place on their dashboard. 
If your placard is lost or stolen, you will need to report it to the District Personnel as soon as possible 
and pay a $25.00 fee to get another one. If anyone shows u p at the District Property displaying the 
lost or stolen placard, they will NOT be allowed access. 
“District Member” Is a parcel owner on which taxes are levied by White Mountain Lakes County 
Recreation District. 
B. Associate Member: To receive your placard, you must go to the Lake Office, provide a bill from 
the Silver Creek Irrigation District, and fill out a form with the following information: 
Member’s Name 
Member’s Phone Number(s) 
Email address 
Property address 
Current Mailing address 
Once your information is verified and you pay the yearly membership fee, you will be issued your 
placard. Your placard is only good for that calendar year. Your placard may be shared with your 
family and guests. You take responsibility for those using your  placard. Guests: If you have other 
guests joining you at the lake you need to notify the District Personnel; identify yourself as an 
Associate Member and provide them with your guest’s information. Your guest(s) will need to stop

<!-- page 469 -->
3 
 
 
at the office with identification and they will be given a day pass to place on their dashboard. If your 
placard is lost or stolen, you will need to report it to the District Personnel as soon as possible and 
pay a 
$25.00 fee to get another one. If anyone shows up at the District property displaying the lost or 
stolen placard, they will NOT be allowed access. 
“Associate Member” is a member of the Silver Creek Irrigation District who provides proof of 
membership. A yearly fee is required and determined by the District. 
1.6 Loud, abusive, threatening, or indecent conduct is not allowed. Excessive noise is  not allowed. 
1.7 Use of the District facilities shall be on a first-come, first-serve basis. 
A.  For large gatherings please contact the lake office a minimum of one week in advance for further 
information on Day Use Passes. 
B. Beach Ramadas may be reserved a minimum of one week in advance. 
1.8 The Silver Creek Irrigation District owns the lake property and has the right to store and use the water in the 
lake for irrigation. The Silver Creek Irrigation District controls the water level in the lake based on irrigatio n 
requirements and the current agreement between the Irrigation District and the Recreation District. Lake level 
and lake surface area will vary. Not all potential hazards are, or can be known. Caution and attention to 
obscured hazards is the responsibility of the user. Silver Creek Irrigation District Agreement refers to the 
written agreement between the White Mountain Lakes County Recreation Improvement District and the 
Silver Creek Irrigation District. The current agreement provides the Recreation District with a minimum lake 
level for White Mountain Lake offering a usable lake surface for recreation and provides the exclusive control 
of the recreation use and improvements on and around White Mountain Lake and Mexican Lake. 
 
2.0 Aircraft and or Drones 
2.1 Operation of aircraft and or drones on District property is not allowed unless preapproved by District 
Personnel. 
 
3.0 Beach and Shoreline Areas 
3.1 Open fires on District property are not allowed. Propane grills only. 
3.2 Excessive horseplay and aggressive behavior are not allowed. 
3.3 Fishing at the swim cove and beach are not allowed. 
3.4 Boats and other watercraft are not allowed in the swim area, as marked by buoys. 
3.5 Motorized vehicles are not allowed from entering the beach area, except as necessary by District or Law 
Enforcement personnel in the performance of their duties. 
3.6 All pedestrian and vehicular travel on ice is not allowed. 
3.7 No glass or ceramic containers of any type in “District Areas, Properties, or Facilities”. 
 
4.0 Boat Ramps and Docks 
4.1 Private Boat Ramps or Docks may not be placed on District property without the written approval of the 
Board of Directors. 
4.2 All watercraft must be removed entirely from the lake when refueling. 
 
5.0 Boating/Water skiing 
5.1 The Arizona Game and Fish Department and the Navajo County Sheriff’s Office has jurisdiction to enforce 
ARS Title 5 - “Amusements and Sports” upon the Lakes. Refer to Boater’s Guide of Arizona available at the 
office. 
5.2 All unmanned watercraft must be removed from the District by sundown. 
5.3 All watercraft shall be launched from an approved boat launch or ramp, except that shoreline property 
owners may cross District property to launch watercraft from their private property, provided: 
A.  District property is not damaged by such private launch.

<!-- page 470 -->
4 
 
 
B. Motor vehicles and/or trailers are not used in the launch. 
C. The launch does not endanger the safety of others. 
5.4 Watercraft slip rental is offered by the District. 
5.5 No watercraft shall be operated in a careless, reckless or negligent manner, or at any speed that is 
unreasonable for existing conditions. 
5.6 Before 10:00 AM, special consideration shall be given to preserve the peace and quiet of neighborhoods 
near the lake, and to accommodate fishermen. No speed over 25 miles per hour.  
5.7 Water skiing is allowed from 10:00 AM to sunset only. 
5.8 Boats pulling skiers must operate in a safe manner and in a counterclockwise direction. 
5.9 Lake level and lake surface area will vary. Not all potential hazards are, or can be known. Caution and 
attention to obscured hazards is the responsibility of the user.  
5.10  Boats and other watercraft are not allowed from entering the swim area, as marked by buoys.  
 
6.0 Camping 
6.1 Any overnight stay on District property which does not involve active use of District recreation, such as 
fishing, is not allowed. 
 
7.0       Children 
7.1       Children 12 years of age and under must be accompanied by an adult (18 years of age or older) at all times. 
 
8.0 Concessions 
8.1 All concessions operated within the District areas must be approved, in writing, by the Board of Directors. 
A.  “Concessions” shall mean any business operated within the areas owned, leased, or controlled by 
the Recreation District for the purpose of profit and/or solicitation of charitable donations. 
 
9.0 Firearms and Explosives 
9.1 Discharge of firearms in the District property is not allowed.  
9.2 Fireworks or other explosives are not allowed unless approved by the Board of Directors. 
 
10.0  Fishing 
10.1  The Lakes and Silver Creek are private. According to the Arizona Game and Fish Department, private lakes 
do not require a fishing license, however, private streams such as Silver Creek do require a fishing license 
except for the section of Silver Creek in the District area.  
10.2  All techniques for “harvesting” fish in quantity are not allowed. 
10.3  A maximum of two (2) fishing poles are allowed per person in the water at any one time. A maximum of two 
(2) hooks shall be allowed per line.  
10.4  Fishing limits are six (6) Catfish, six (6) Bass, and six (6) Trout per day, per person. There is no limit for 
Carp.  
10.5  Cleaning fish on District property is not allowed except in designated areas. 
10.6  A fishing dock is available for fishing. 
10.7  No fishing in swim area.  
A.   “Swim Area” shall mean the area located north of the boat ramp that has been improved for 
swimming activities, to include shore and water within the area marked by buoys. 
 
11.0  Littering 
11.1  Deposit of trash, garbage, human or animal waste collected from a private residence or a commercial 
business into receptacles provided by the District is  not allowed. 
11.2  Dumping any foreign liquid or materials on District property is not allowed.

<!-- page 471 -->
5 
 
 
12.0  Motor Vehicles 
12.1  Unauthorized motor vehicles shall not be driven on the shore of the lakes within the high-water mark. except 
as necessary by District or Law Enforcement personnel in the performance of their duties. 
 
13.0  Pets 
13.1  Dogs, cats, and other pets shall be kept under physical restraint at all times. Leashes must not exceed six (6) 
feet. 
13.2  All animal waste must be collected promptly and removed by the person responsible for the animal. 
13.3  No horses or horse trailers are allowed in the parking area or on the beach. 
 
14.0  Swimming 
14.1  Swimming is not allowed within one hundred (100) feet of docks, fishing docks or boat ramp. 
14.2  There is no Life Guard. All swimming is at the users’ own risk. 
14.3  Boats and other watercraft are not allowed in the swim area. 
 
15.0  Vandalism 
15.1  Vandalism of any kind is not allowed and may result in suspension of the use privileges for the 
individual(s) involved. 
15.2  Destroying trees or plants and/or removing limbs for any purpose is not allowed. 
 
 
DEFINITIONS 
 
A-1 “Board of Directors” shall mean the White Mountain Lakes County Recreation Improvement District 
Board of Directors which is the Navajo County Board of Supervisors. 
 
A-2 “District” shall mean the White Mountain Lakes County Recreation Improvement District. 
 
A-3 “District Areas, Property or Facilities” shall mean areas or property owned, leased, or controlled by the 
District. 
 
A-4 “District Personnel” In an effort to enhance public safety awareness and the application of these Rules and 
Regulations, District Personnel are employed as representatives of, and to act on behalf of, the District. These 
staff members look forward to working with you for a safe and enjoyable day at the lake. 
 
A-5 “Silver Creek Irrigation District” shall mean an organization of farmers or ranchers who own and/or 
control certain lands and/or water rights.  
A-6 “Watercraft” means any boat designed to be propelled by machinery, oars, paddles or wind action upon a 
sail for navigation on the water.
